# Habit-Bull: Daily Goal Planner (App Store ID 1041482672) — Full Review Analysis

**App:** Habit-Bull (HabitBull) — *Daily Goal Planner · Best To Do List · Streak Tracker*
**Developer (per `manifest.json`):** AppForge Inc. · bundle `oristats.HabitBull`
**Reviews analysed:** 2,024 — every single one · 82 storefronts · 9 Nov 2015 → 6 Aug 2026 (130 calendar months)
**Corpus extracted:** 8 Sep 2026 (`manifest.json`) · **written-review mean 4.05** (1,291×5★, 227×4★, 122×3★, 81×2★, 303×1★)
**Store-level context:** from this repository's ranking snapshot `habit-apps-ranked.json` — **20,955 public ratings at 4.65**, ranked in 52 storefronts, best search rank 6, median rank 21.5, audience "moderate". No live store page was fetched (§1.6).

---

## How to read this

Every claim carries a count, a % of a stated denominator, a signal label and real review IDs, all resolvable in `app store review/55. Habit-Bull - Daily Goal Planner - Best To Do List Streak Tracker/reviews.jsonl`. **Part 10 indexes all 2,024 reviews individually**, so any number traces back to exact reviews in one step, and any review traces forward to the themes it carries. **Appendix C lists every review ID behind every theme.**

| Band | Label | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| < 0.1% | Ignore | Not promoted unless safety/legal/data-loss |
| 0.1 – 0.5% | Weak signal | Recorded, cautious wording |
| 0.5 – 1% | Emerging | Worth investigating |
| 1 – 3% | Meaningful | Strong candidate |
| 3 – 5% | Very strong | Should shape roadmap |
| > 5% | High-priority | Strong problem or opportunity |

Theme counts are **non-exclusive** — 4,216 assignments over 2,024 reviews (2.08 per review, median 2). Global percentages always divide by **2,024**; segment, country and era rates are labelled as such and shown with the global count. Composite "unions" count each review once.

Quotes are given in the original language first; English translations are the analyst's and are marked *EN*.

### ⚠️ Eight warnings before any number here is used

1. **The written corpus is more negative than the public rating, and the gap is uneven.** Written reviews are 9.66% of public ratings (2,024 of 20,955), and their mean (4.05) sits **0.60★ below** the public average (4.65). The gap is 0.14★ in Australia but **1.07★ in the UK and 1.18★ in Brazil** (§7.9). Written reviews over-represent the moments when something broke.
2. **Review volume collapsed after 2020.** 399 reviews in 2016, 288 in 2020, 60 in 2021, 16 in 2026 (to 6 Aug). The five eras in Part 8 hold 709 / 400 / 597 / 189 / 129 reviews. One review moves an E5 share by 0.78 percentage points.
3. **This is an English-dominant corpus.** 82 storefronts, but the US holds 43.53% and six English-primary storefronts hold 1,310 reviews (64.72%). Only 67 reviews (3.31%) are in non-Latin scripts (Appendix F). Six storefronts clear 50 reviews (§7.1).
4. **No version field exists.** Every release, price change and incident in Part 8 is dated from what reviewers say and when they say it. Two reviewers quote version numbers (1.4.7, 1.4.11 — `6706410264`, `7097957463`).
5. **14.48% of the corpus carries no product experience** (`META_LOWINFO` 261 + `META_PREUSE` 32 = 293, mean 4.89). Median body length is 111 characters.
6. **Launch and press bursts exist, and the app asks for ratings.** The busiest day is 10 Nov 2015 (21 reviews), the second day on the store, when reviewers cite a Reddit post (`1284433048`, `1308758985`). January 2016 holds 105 reviews, with BuzzFeed cited (`1322958708`, `1327696946`). Nine reviews mention the in-app rating prompt (`META_PROMPT` 9 (0.44%), weak), and one 2024 reviewer alleges the positive reviews are bought (`11359320730`). None of this is proof of manipulation; it is disclosed in §1.7.
7. **24 reviews (1.19%, meaningful) contradict their own rating** — 19 of them are 5★ reviews describing a failure. They are kept and listed in full (§5.6, Appendix E).
8. **Prices are reviewer-reported.** Reviewers describe a one-time premium of about $4–5 until June 2018, then an annual subscription of about $19.99 (`2640020936`, `2644893532`). Local-currency figures vary. `is_edited` is true for only 4 records; 121 records (5.98%) have helpful votes (maximum 6).

---

# PART 0 — READ THIS FIRST

**Executive summary in one line:** Habit-Bull is praised, consistently for a decade, as a **simple, flexible tracker** — yes/no or numeric habits, "N times a week" goals, streak calendars and a generous five-habit free tier (design praise [U:design_praise] 568 (28.06%); tracking-model praise [U:tracking_core] 346 (17.09%)). Its complaints are not about that core. They come from **what happened around it**: four crash waves after updates, a paid "sync" that reviewers say does not work, a 2018 switch from a one-time fee to an annual subscription that left earlier buyers asking to be honoured, a July 2020 upsell that appeared on every launch with a close button that started a purchase, and a support inbox reviewers describe as silent. **Paying reviewers are the unhappiest group in the corpus: `PAY_BOUGHT` = 165 (8.15%), mean 2.56, and 96 of them (58.2%) give 1–2★.**

## 0.1 The people who paid are the people who were let down

**`PAY_BOUGHT` = 165 (8.15%, high-priority, mean 2.56).** The paid mean fell in every era: 4.24 at launch (E1, 38 payers) → 2.78 (E2, 37) → 1.91 (E3, 56) → 1.41 (E4, 27) (§6.1).

Within the paid segment (segment rates, denominator 165):

| What payers report | n among paid | Segment rate | Global n | Share of that theme's reviews that come from payers |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Data & account continuity failure (sync, login, lost data, lost purchase) | 69 | 41.8% | 143 | 48.3% |
| Crashes | 44 | 26.7% | 197 | 22.3% |
| Support does not reply | 42 | 25.5% | 83 | 50.6% |
| Sync between devices fails | 36 | 21.8% | 62 | 58.1% |
| Asks for a refund | 17 | 10.3% | 19 | 89.5% |
| Purchase lost on reinstall / new phone | 15 | 9.1% | 18 | 83.3% |

The headline paid feature, by reviewers' account, was cross-device sync. `4823714493` (us, 3★, Sep 2019): *"I bought the premium because I wanted to sync. Can't sync anywhere and app says no signal."* `5384533156` (gb, 1★, Jan 2020): *"Paid £17.49 to sync phone/iPad/watch and it doesn't work. No response from app developer and Apple won't help."* `4956440235` (us, 1★, Oct 2019): *"if you subscribe you wont be able to sync across your devices !!! So by any chance if you change phone or reset your device YOU WILL NOT GET BACK YOUR DATA."*

## 0.2 The subscription switch, then the upsell that bought for you

**Subscription-model backlash [U:sub_backlash] 82 (4.05%, very strong, mean 1.91)** — price-rise notes, subscription objections, earlier buyers not honoured, the every-launch pop-up and "forced to pay" reports.

| Era (Part 8) | Reviews | Monetization friction | Subscription backlash | Reliability failure | Mean ★ |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| E1 · Nov 2015–Jun 2017 | 709 | 7 (1.0%) | 0 (0.0%) | 46 (6.5%) | 4.67 |
| E2 · Jul 2017–May 2018 | 400 | 28 (7.0%) | 0 (0.0%) | 105 (26.2%) | 3.99 |
| E3 · Jun 2018–Jun 2020 | 597 | 57 (9.5%) | 18 (3.0%) | 179 (30.0%) | 3.84 |
| **E4 · Jul 2020–Jun 2021** | 189 | **67 (35.4%)** | **55 (29.1%)** | **79 (41.8%)** | **2.72** |
| E5 · Jul 2021–Aug 2026 | 129 | 12 (9.3%) | 9 (7.0%) | 25 (19.4%) | 3.72 |

- **June 2018 — one-time fee becomes a subscription.** `2640020936` (us, 1★, 4 Jun 2018): *"It was 4.99$ to unlock the premium features, now it's 19$ a year! From a license model to a subscription model, and about 400% increase in price, and all you get while updating? "Changes"."* `PAY_PRICE_HIGH` = 42 (2.08%, meaningful, mean 2.71); 40 of the 42 are from June 2018 on.
- **Earlier buyers asked to pay again** — `PAY_REGRESS` = 9 (0.44%, weak, **mean 1.33**), Dec 2019 → Jan 2025. `5900039933` (us, 1★, May 2020): *"I bought HabitBull for a fixed price before they moved to a subscription model. I tried contacting them several times to ask them to honor my membership. Absolutely no reply, not one."*
- **July 2020 — the pop-up.** `PAY_NAG` = 44 (2.17%, meaningful, mean 2.02), first seen 5 Jul 2020, still reported in Dec 2024. `PAY_FORCED` = 20 (0.99%, emerging, **mean 1.25**), all 10 Jul 2020 → 3 Jan 2021. In July 2020 alone, 35 of 68 reviews (51.5%) carry one or both codes. `6194515737` (us, 4★, Jul 2020): *"I clicked on the x at the top, but instead of giving me the data screen, it acted like I'd tried to make a purchase."* `6217825775` (gb, 1★, Jul 2020): *"The pop up has a fake 'X' in the top corner to close the message, which if you press, causes your account to agree to the premium subscription."*

## 0.3 Four crash waves, each tied to an update

**`BUG_CRASH` = 197 (9.73%, high-priority, mean 2.04).** Reliability failure [U:reliability] 434 (21.44%, high-priority, mean 2.19).

| Window | Reviews that month | Crash reviews | Concentration | Representative IDs |
|---|---:|---:|---|---|
| Jul 2017 — update crashes on launch | 47 | 12 | all 12 in the UK storefront | `1673186816` `1693303166` `1700889647` |
| Jan 2018 — registration hangs, "buy premium" crashes | 62 | 9 (+14 `ACCT_BUG`, 10 `PAY_IAP_FAIL`) | us 33 of 62 | `2061252128` `2062646143` `2073281420` |
| Nov 2018 — iOS 12, app closes on open | 45 | 24 | 23 of the 24 in Brazil | `3385514055` `3392621280` `3398953265` |
| Oct 2020 – Feb 2021 — launch crash after the 2020 updates | 70 | 30 | 26 of the 30 in the UK | `6706410264` `6948589393` `7097957463` |

`BUG_UPDATE` = 43 (2.12%, meaningful, mean 1.91). Fixes were reported twice (`1707578570`, `7097957463`), the second after *"nearly nine months of complaints"*.

## 0.4 Support silence and visible neglect

**`SUP_BAD` = 83 (4.10%, very strong, mean 1.45)** — 66 of the 83 are 1★. **Neglect signals [U:neglect] 156 (7.71%, high-priority, mean 1.95)** add `DEV_ABANDON` = 42 (2.08%) and `BUG_COMPAT` = 53 (2.62%): no iPhone X layout from November 2017 (`1923610880`) until a December 2020 update fixed it (`6718923959`). `3535806272` (us, 1★, Dec 2018): *"It's been out for over a year. Fix it."* `12574094084` (us, 2★, Apr 2025): *"The last update was 9 months ago, this is unacceptable for a subscription based product."*

## 0.5 What works: a flexible tracker that changes behaviour

- **Simple and quick** — `DES_CLEAN` = 475 (23.47%, high-priority, mean 4.89).
- **Flexible goal definitions** — `CORE_FLEX` = 148 (7.31%, high-priority, mean 4.82). `1327696946` (us, 5★, Feb 2016): *"You set your own standards for the habits (every day, certain days a week, or a number of days in a week/month). I like this edit because it doesn't disqualify you if you don't do each habit every single day."*
- **Stats and streaks** — `STAT_GOOD` = 187 (9.24%), `CORE_STREAK` = 75 (3.71%).
- **Reported outcomes** — `OUT_LIFE` = 151 (7.46%, high-priority, mean 4.89). `5361919157` (us, 5★, Jan 2020): *"I've been a nail biter for 26 years but getting to tap "yes!" everyday is exactly what I needed to finally stop."* `4608093672` (il, 5★, Aug 2019): *"I've done over 10K pushups and it's all because I don't want to "disappoint the app""*
- **Motivation layer** [U:motivation] 362 (17.89%) — quirky messages, streaks and a forum.

## 0.6 The five-habit free tier is a differentiator and the upgrade trigger

**`PAY_FREE_OK` = 96 (4.74%, very strong, mean 4.86).** Reviewers compare it favourably with three-habit free tiers elsewhere (`3363102377`, `3728440907`). **`PAY_CAP` = 87 (4.30%, very strong, mean 3.98)** — the same limit is one of the two reasons payers most often name for upgrading, beside sync (§6.2; `1308688562`, `1335999992`, `1339211456`) and, for others, the reason not to (`2193551017`, `14276683948`). `2090700914` (nl, 5★, Jan 2018): *"Geen hinderlijke beperkingen of voortdurende druk om over te gaan. De makers nemen de free gebruiker ook serieus"* — *EN:* "No annoying restrictions or constant pressure to upgrade. The makers take the free user seriously too." That sentence describes the product the July 2020 pop-up replaced.

## 0.7 The app never reached the rest of the Apple ecosystem

**Device reach gaps [U:device_reach] 113 (5.58%, high-priority, mean 3.15)**: no iPad layout (`IPAD` = 30, 1.48%), no Apple Watch, Mac or web app (`PLATFORM_WANT` = 24, 1.19%), no widget (`WIDGET_WANT` = 16, 0.79%), and the late new-screen layout above. Some reviewers left for this reason (`2013414846`, `3257491999`).

## 0.8 Small brand irritants that cost ratings

**Brand irritants [U:brand_irritants] 30 (1.48%, meaningful, mean 3.03)**: the launch-screen photo of a woman hiker (`DES_SPLASH` = 20, 0.99%, 2016–2022), the app icon (`DES_ICON` = 6) and pet-name messages (`MOT_MSG_NEG` = 4). `1604334907` (us, 1★, May 2017): *"I purchased premium. I am uninstalling due to lack of dev response to the main photo of the girl."* `5378082186` (us, 2★, Jan 2020): *"Deleted when the reminder called me "cutie"."*

## 0.9 The five decisions this corpus supports

| # | Decision | Evidence | Part 9 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Make what people paid for work** — sync, restore and login — before any new paid feature | Paid segment: data & account failure 69 of 165; data & account union 143 (7.07%) | F1, F6 |
| 2 | **Remove the every-launch upsell and any close control that can start a purchase** | `PAY_NAG` 44, `PAY_FORCED` 20; E4 mean 2.72 | F2, M5 |
| 3 | **Offer a one-time or lifetime option and honour earlier one-time buyers** | `PAY_REGRESS` 9 at mean 1.33; `PAY_SUB_NEG` 19; `PAY_PRICE_HIGH` 42 | M1 |
| 4 | **Keep the five-habit free tier** — it is praised more often than it is resented | `PAY_FREE_OK` 96 vs negative `PAY_CAP` mentions | M2 |
| 5 | **Ship a release process that stops launch crashes and a support channel that answers** | `BUG_CRASH` 197; `SUP_BAD` 83; four dated waves | F4, F5 |

# PART 1 — SCOPE AND METHOD

## 1.1 Files used

| File | Role | Records |
|---|---|---|
| `app store review/55. Habit-Bull - Daily Goal Planner - Best To Do List Streak Tracker/reviews.jsonl` | Primary corpus — every review read | 2,024 |
| `.../by_country/*.jsonl` | 82 per-storefront files | 2,024 (reconciled, §1.3) |
| `.../manifest.json` | Extraction metadata, per-country counts, rating distribution, mean | — |
| `.../_state.json` | Per-storefront crawl completeness | 134 storefronts polled, all `complete=true` |
| `habit-apps-ranked.json` (repo root) | Public rating counts, averages, search ranks and market tiers per storefront (§1.6, Appendix G) | — |

## 1.2 Schema actually present

Every record carries 13 fields, uniformly: `review_id` · `app_id` · `app_name` · `country` · `country_name` · `rating` · `title` · `body` · `author` · `date` (ISO-8601 UTC) · `vote_count` · `vote_sum` · `is_edited`.

- **Used for findings:** `review_id`, `country`, `rating`, `title`, `body`, `date`, `vote_count`, `vote_sum`, `is_edited`.
- **Present but unused:** `author` (pseudonymous; never aggregated), `app_id`, `app_name`, `country_name`.
- **`is_edited` is true for 4 records** (`1707578570`, `5772353903`, `6687908842`, `7097957463`). Several more reviews say in their text that they were updated (`2640020936`, `2644893532`, `4174090943`).
- **`vote_count` > 0 on 121 records (5.98%).** Maximum 6 (`5501033289`). Votes are never used as weights.
- **Body length:** median 111 characters, mean 164.0.

## 1.3 Coverage and reconciliation

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Lines in `reviews.jsonl` | 2,024 |
| Unique `review_id` | 2,024 (no duplicate IDs) |
| `manifest.json` `total_reviews` | 2,024 — match |
| Sum of `manifest.json` per-country counts | 2,024 — match |
| Rows across the 82 `by_country/*.jsonl` files | 2,024; union of IDs = 2,024 |
| IDs in country files but not in `reviews.jsonl` / vice versa | 0 / 0 |
| Per-country count mismatches (file vs main vs manifest vs `_state.json`) | 0 |
| `_state.json` complete flag | true for all 134 polled storefronts (82 with reviews) |
| Rating distribution vs manifest | exact match (5:1,291 · 4:227 · 3:122 · 2:81 · 1:303) |
| Mean rating vs manifest (4.048) | exact match (4.0484) |
| Records classified in `temp/55-review-classification.py` | **2,024 (100%)** |
| Classified IDs not present in the corpus | 0 |
| Corpus records with no theme assigned | 0 |
| Duplicate keys in the map / intra-review duplicate themes | 0 / 0 |
| Undefined theme codes used | 0 |
| Theme assignments total | 4,216 (2.08 per review) |
| Declared-but-unused codes | 0 of 103 |

Reproducible with `python3 temp/55-reconcile.py` and `python3 temp/55-validate.py`.

## 1.4 Deduplication rule

**No records were removed.** Exactly one title+body pair repeats: "Good :: Good" from Mexico (16 May 2017, `1615596534`) and Vietnam (25 May 2020, `5989825005`). They have different storefronts, dates and IDs, so both are kept. Every percentage uses **2,024** as the denominator.

## 1.5 Processing method

1. **Parse** all 2,024 records. Sort deterministically by date, then `review_id`, to assign a working index 1–2,024 (`temp/55-index.tsv`). Reconcile against `by_country/`, `manifest.json` and `_state.json`.
2. **Read every review individually, in the original language**, in chronological order. The corpus was split into 17 files of up to 125 reviews (`temp/55-chunks/001–017.txt`) and read end to end: no sampling, no keyword filtering, no skipped chunk. English, Portuguese, Spanish, Catalan, German, French, Dutch, Danish, Swedish, Polish, Czech, Italian, Turkish, Vietnamese, Russian, Ukrainian, Chinese (Simplified and Traditional), Japanese, Korean, Arabic and Malayalam reviews were read as written.
3. **Hand-assign themes** from a 103-code, 12-family taxonomy (Appendix B) built inductively while reading. Codes were added as new patterns appeared, and the running codebook (`temp/55-codebook-notes.md`) records when. Earlier chunks were revisited when a later code applied to them (for example `USER_COACH`, `PAY_REGRET`, `MOT_WANT`). One line per review (`temp/55-cls/NNN.txt`: `index CODE,CODE`). Assignment is non-exclusive.
4. **Resolve index → review_id** deterministically (`temp/55-build-classification.py`), asserting that the index table matches `reviews.jsonl`. This emits `temp/55-review-classification.py`.
5. **Validate programmatically** (`temp/55-validate.py`): zero unknown IDs, zero unassigned records, zero duplicate keys, zero intra-review duplicate themes, zero undefined codes.
6. **Cross-check against text** after classification. Reviews whose text mentions crash, sync, pop-up, refund, subscription, login, dark mode, iPhone X, iPad, widget, Apple Watch, premium or support but lack the matching code were listed and re-read. Nearly all were negations or other senses ("doesn't crash", "Crushing my goals", "iPhone X support"). Two were real misses and were fixed (`2070350968` gained `PAY_IAP_FAIL`, `11359320730` gained `PAY_BOUGHT`).
7. **Aggregate** (`temp/55-aggregate.py`, `temp/55-extra.py`): global, per-star, per-storefront, market-tier, year, era and month counts; composite unions; paid-segment lifts.
8. **Text sub-cuts are always taken *inside* a hand-coded theme** — for example price figures inside `PAY_*` reviews. They are labelled as text sub-cuts wherever they appear. No finding rests on a keyword search over the whole corpus.

**No automated classifier was used.** The error risk is judgement error on short or ambiguous reviews; the calls most likely to move a number are disclosed in Appendix D.

## 1.6 Limitations — read these before citing any figure

1. **No live store data.** No App Store page or developer site was fetched. **Every price, tier, trial and feature description is reconstructed from review text** and carries reviewers' own errors. Two reviewers say "$20 a month" (`3278313008`, `5544529848`) where everyone else says a year. Contradictions are reported, not resolved.
2. **One external source is used:** this repository's `habit-apps-ranked.json` snapshot (undated in-file). It supplies public rating counts, averages, search ranks and the `market_tier` label (`rich` / `volume`), which §7.2 adopts as its high-spend definition.
3. **Selection bias.** Writers are 9.66% of raters and average 0.60★ lower (§7.9). Incidents produce bursts of 1★ writers who would otherwise never review.
4. **Small late samples.** July 2021 – August 2026 holds 129 reviews, and 2023 alone holds 16. Late-era trends are described, not quantified precisely.
5. **No developer responses are in the data.** Reviewers mention replies and canned answers (`6360079136`, `7059744677`, `7097957463`); the replies themselves are not available.
6. **Storefront ≠ language ≠ nationality.** Spanish text appears in the UK storefront (`14394403203`) and English text in many non-English storefronts. Country findings describe storefronts.
7. **"Paid" means the reviewer says they paid.** `PAY_BOUGHT` includes one-time buyers, subscribers and trial converts. The same reviewer's purchase cannot be verified, and conversion rates cannot be inferred.

## 1.7 Solicited reviews, bursts and review integrity

- **Launch burst.** 38 reviews in launch week (9–15 Nov 2015) and 21 on 10 Nov 2015 alone, with reviewers citing a Reddit launch post and a developer who spoke with them there (`1284433048`, `1308758985`, `1317049655`). They read as organic but motivated early adopters: 137 reviews in 2015 average 4.71.
- **Press bursts.** January 2016 holds 105 reviews (mean 4.77), with BuzzFeed and a TV review cited (`1317786863`, `1322958708`, `1327696946`). October 2019 and October 2020 carry three Korean reviews that say a rapper recommended the app (`4905479317`, `4912823642`, `6520274563`).
- **A developer promotion.** One 2016 reviewer received the full version free (`1322768678`, `PAY_GIFT` 1).
- **The rating prompt.** `META_PROMPT` = 9 (0.44%, weak, mean 2.44). Complaints: *"You have asked me 4 time within 1 minute to rate your app"* (`1956880711`); a lock screen and rating dialog that trapped a user (`1675008191`); *"Stop asking for a review"* (`7738946654`). One Brazilian found it cute (`1378213453`). The prompt plausibly inflates short 5★ reviews; `META_LOWINFO` reviews average 4.89. That is a hypothesis, not a measured effect.
- **Negative burst.** 12 Jul 2020 holds 11 reviews, all during the pop-up backlash (§8.4).
- **Fake-review allegation:** one (`11359320730`), unverified.
- **Contradictions:** 24 (1.19%) — §5.6 and Appendix E.
- **Spam and jokes:** one advert for a coaching service (`1528497631`) and one joke review (`1758524697`). Both are kept and coded.

---

# PART 2 — PRODUCT AND MONETIZATION MODEL (review-derived)

## 2.1 What the product is, per reviewers

| Capability | How reviewers describe it | Representative IDs |
|---|---|---|
| Habit types | Yes/no habits or numeric habits (minutes, pages, glasses, cigarettes) | `1286107824` `1325376912` `1813721000` |
| Success rules | "At least", "exactly" or "not more than" a target | `1325376912` `2487407176` `5412230040` |
| Frequencies | Daily, specific weekdays, N days per week; weekly is the only period option for "N days per period" | `1327696946` `1358157955` `10780904796` |
| Goal length | A target number of days (reviewers mention 66); an end date is required | `1367368690` `2734060777` `5556749525` |
| Views | Daily, weekly, monthly and yearly tabs with a flip transition; calendar chains | `1361211433` `1302591393` `5503142349` |
| Stats | Success percentage, streaks, charts, totals | `1327346587` `1938145606` `4877365239` |
| Notes | A note on any day's entry | `1328953983` `1598952847` `4498607051` |
| Past entries | Log earlier days, including before install | `1538068134` `1837322644` `5426305783` |
| Reminders | Timed reminders per habit, several a day later on; humorous wording; badge countdown | `5251051111` `2210985599` `6314169224` |
| Motivation | Quirky messages, motivational quotes and posters, milestone cheers | `1345453065` `5836293884` `8992568406` |
| Community | A discussion forum per habit type | `1434336181` `1514813712` `12098769011` |
| Customisation | Colour coding, categories, inactive habits | `1342058264` `2372700165` `8992568406` |
| Security | Passcode lock; Touch ID requested | `1326179122` `1463966723` `1675008191` |
| Account & sync | Optional account (email, Facebook; later Apple, Google); cloud sync; manual sync button | `1349318323` `2056774463` `13273003532` |
| Export | CSV / Excel export | `1728971383` `2573453386` `7224132499` |
| Platforms | iPhone app plus an Android version; iPad runs the stretched phone app; no Watch, Mac or web app | `1925943692` `6218849398` `10825023143` |
| Appearance | Launch-screen photo, flip animations, dark mode added mid-2020 with unreadable text | `5870204615` `6209826711` `7975946217` |

## 2.2 What it costs, per reviewers — a ten-year price history

| Period | Free tier | Premium | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| Nov 2015 – mid 2016 | 5 habits | One-time **$3.99–$4** | `1287413103` `1313500633` `1314774160` `1322205409` |
| mid 2016 – May 2018 | 5 habits | One-time **$4.99** (£4.99; CA$6.99; "7.99 dollars" in Australia; DKK 45); "up to 100" habits, sync, CSV export | `1401096756` `1702393299` `2070142190` `2078145265` `2220539344` `2399403744` |
| **Jun 2018 on** | 5 habits, "ad supported" | **Annual subscription ~$19.99** (£17.49–£18, €20, ~R$70+) with a 7-day trial | `2640020936` `2644893532` `3591335378` `3723488309` `4332329392` `5384533156` |
| **Jul 2020 on** | 5 habits; full-screen premium pop-up on every launch | Same subscription; reviewers report ~$30 AUD and 30€ | `6177064571` `6180444776` `6850581887` `7305967703` |
| 2023 – 2025 | 5 habits | 10-day trial reported; one reviewer says monthly billing | `9994781256` `12241472265` |

**Text sub-cut (inside `PAY_*` reviews only):** 16 reviews name a $3.99–$4.99 / £4.99 price, dated Jan 2016 → Feb 2020 (the later ones recall the old price). 30 name a $19.99–$30 / £17–18 / €20–30 annual price, dated Jun 2018 → Mar 2022.

## 2.3 Free / paid / trial / unclear classification

| Capability | Status | Basis |
|---|---|---|
| Up to 5 habits, all habit types, views, stats, reminders, notes on free habits | **Free** | `1314254670` `1519990236` `1545235290` `2147288846` |
| More than 5 habits | **Paid** (one-time until Jun 2018, then subscription) | `1308688562` `4755264918` `14276683948` |
| Cross-device cloud sync | **Paid** (reviewers call it "paid for sync") | `1677887879` `1815881524` `4871183371` |
| CSV export | **Paid** | `1330390028` `2573453386` `4962257674` |
| No full-screen upsell / no ads | **Paid** (from Jun 2018 ads; from Jul 2020 every-launch pop-up) | `2644893532` `6866823641` `12045845669` |
| Trial | **Trial-gated** 7 days (2018–2020), 10 days (2023) | `3591335378` `6478833374` `9994781256` |
| Notes on free tier | **Unclear** — one free user's notes disappeared | `3632717994` |
| Additional themes | **Unclear** — asked for as a premium add-on | `2644893532` |
| Access to data without paying (Jul 2020 – Jan 2021) | **Unclear** — 20 reviewers say the app demanded payment to open; others dismissed the pop-up and continued | `6189130796` `6189489425` `6212428142` |

## 2.4 One structural observation about the model

The premium bundle has always been **"more habits + sync + export"**. More habits costs the developer nothing to deliver. Sync and export depend on servers and upkeep — and those are the two paid features reviewers most often report as broken (`SYNC_BUG` = 62 (3.06%); `EXPORT` = 15 (0.74%), of which 5 are failures). Moving to a subscription in 2018 changed what payers expect: an annual fee reads as a promise of ongoing development. Reviewers then measured the app by its update log (`4306446749`, `5512997233`, `12574094084`). `3713152341` (us, 3★, Jan 2019): *"It's not like a yoga or meditation app, which is constantly adding new videos/content, so why a yearly fee?!?"*

## 2.5 Positioning versus the reviewing population

Reviewers came from paper, spreadsheets and competitors: Strides, Way of Life, Productive, coach.me, Streaks, Todoist, Habit List (`USER_SWITCH` = 194 (9.58%), mean 4.68 — `1337205851`, `1344986445`, `1369720640`, `3622687510`). They accept its looks for its function — *"I'll take function over form any day"* (`2590464683`; similarly `1313140033`, `7873992832`). Against 3-habit free tiers it wins on generosity (`2458999867`, `3363102377`). Against subscription competitors, the one-time price was the pitch until 2018 (`1429720658`, `1969268466`, `2078145265`). Specialist uses appear: therapists and a clinical psychologist recommend it to clients (`1508953907`, `2054775005`, `8292350972`), and quit-habit trackers use it for smoking, alcohol, nail-biting and NoFap (`5236944006`, `5361919157`, `6087663524`).

## 3.3 Prioritised negative themes

### N1 — Crashes — `BUG_CRASH` = 197 (9.73%), high-priority, mean 2.04
107 of the 197 are 1★. They come in four dated waves (§8.4), mostly "closes on launch" after an app or iOS update, plus crashes on specific actions: tapping a month view (`1587511101`, `3384597538`), a particular date (`3097937918`, `3627820544`), adding a seventh habit (`3325234881`), buying premium (§3.3 N6). Reinstalling to escape a crash often wiped data (`1689434162`, `1853242496`, `4491691781`). IDs: `1306549310` `1700889647` `3385514055` `5852404059` `6948589393` `14165307603`.

### N2 — Data & account continuity — [U:data_account] 143 (7.07%), high-priority, mean 1.71
- `SYNC_BUG` = 62 (3.06%, very strong, mean 1.90) — sync partial, silent, or "only half" the data (`1677887879`, `3207863959`), a buried manual button (`1349318323`, `2224962843`), later "servers are dead" (`4871183371`).
- `ACCT_BUG` = 57 (2.82%, meaningful, mean 1.68) — "email invalid" (`1454040306`, `1900017707`), endless spinner (`2062646143`, `5291016068`), reset emails that never arrive (`2220539344`, `2565543536`, `5872007760`).
- `BUG_DATA` = 35 (1.73%, meaningful, **mean 1.31**) — `2695796731` (us, 1★, Jun 2018): *"now I lost my account including years worth of data"*.
- `PAY_RESTORE` = 18 (0.89%, emerging, mean 1.11) — premium gone after a new phone or reinstall (`2168102167`, `3751363151`, `6503407436`).

### N3 — Support silence — `SUP_BAD` = 83 (4.10%), very strong, mean 1.45
Present from January 2016 (`1318911848`) to April 2026 (`13975567006`). Its share rises from 1.1% of E1 reviews to 10.1% of E4 (§8.3). Specifics: a developer site returning errors (`1700889647`, `3794237455`, `6247799179`), a contact form that won't send (`4606789753`), ticket numbers with no reply (`6706410264`), canned answers (`7059744677`), and a "crude response" that closed a suggestion (`6360079136`).

### N4 — Subscription backlash — [U:sub_backlash] 82 (4.05%), very strong, mean 1.91
See §0.2 and Part 6. `PAY_NAG` = 44 (2.17%), `PAY_FORCED` = 20 (0.99%), `PAY_SUB_NEG` = 19 (0.94%), `PAY_REGRESS` = 9 (0.44%), `PAY_PRICE_RISE` = 6 (0.30%). Also `PAY_PRICE_HIGH` = 42 (2.08%, meaningful, mean 2.71), which is not in the union because some price complaints predate the subscription.

### N5 — Neglect: no updates, no new-screen support — `DEV_ABANDON` = 42 (2.08%) · `BUG_COMPAT` = 53 (2.62%)
Neglect was noticed as early as June 2016 (`1400301314`: *"No updates have been issued in over half a year and the developers are no longer answering emails or responding to questions in the HabitBull forums"*). It became the dominant frame in 2019 (`4497695218` *"Has this been abandoned?"*; `4925876017`; `4956440235`). `BUG_COMPAT` runs 8 Jul 2017 → 13 Aug 2020, mostly the iPhone X+ layout; 17 of its 53 reviews are 4★, which makes it a ratings drag rather than a churn driver (`3311384193`, `3714499359`, `3953087862`).

### N6 — Can't buy, can't register (early 2018) — `PAY_IAP_FAIL` = 14 (0.69%) · `ACCT_BUG` (Jan 2018 share)
All 14 `PAY_IAP_FAIL` reviews date Oct 2016 → Jan 2018, and 10 of them fall in January 2018. These are lost sales stated in reviewers' own words: *"I want to give you money! I want more habits! But you're app crashes whenever I try to upgrade."* (`2006764168`); *"I want to pay you guys money but I just can't!!!"* (`1923709095`).

### N7 — Wrong numbers in the tracker — `BUG_STREAK` = 11 (0.54%, emerging) · `CORE_EDIT` = 7 (0.35%, weak)
Streaks reset at the year change in 2018 and 2020 (`2045909509`, `2070476668`, `5368275407`). "Not more than" goals are saved as "at least" (`5468748878`, `7768440038`, `8557099798`) — for quit-smoking and drinking goals this inverts success. `8557099798` (au, 3★, Apr 2022): *"'Smoke not more than 10 cigarettes'. But when you go into track progress of these goals, they actually show as requiring you to do the task 'at least' that many times"*. Editing a frequency rewrites history (`5913550190`). Since 2026 habits cannot be deleted or deactivated (`13899897825`, `14081068315`, `14101520315`). Small counts, but these break the core promise of accurate tracking.

### N8 — Dated, non-iOS-like UI — `DES_UX_NEG` = 67 (3.31%), very strong, mean 3.45
Mixed direction in practice: 41 of the 67 are 4–5★. Complaints: flip transitions (`3833781377`, `5503142349`, `5870204615`), "looks like an app you'd find on the iPhone 3G" (`2836942295`), small toggles (`4503187234`), numeric entry (`4182020657`, `12926808321`). Many reviewers accept it: *"Functional but ugly"* (`7873992832`).

### N9 — Dark mode unreadable (mid-2020) — `BUG_DARK` = 10 (0.49%), weak, mean 1.90
All between 29 Jun and 7 Aug 2020: *"black writing on the black background"* (`6209826711`; also `6134366309`, `6271175390`).

### N10 — Privacy and account pressure — `PRIV` = 9 (0.44%), weak, mixed
Concerns: premium requires an email account or the purchase is lost (`2064388428`), cloud storage of goal data (`1602965339`), a registration screen first (`5400671798`), and "categories ... important to Big Brother" (`12577935798`). Praise: no login needed (`4109413334`, `5099378918`, `8755695551`).

## 3.4 Prioritised positive themes

### P1 — Simple, quick, clean — `DES_CLEAN` = 475 (23.47%), high-priority, mean 4.89
The single largest theme in every era and in all six eligible storefronts. Its share falls from 31.3% of E1 reviews to 10.1% of E4 and back to 20.9% of E5 (§8.3). IDs: `1284379859` `1308793506` `5938450480` `13462495015`.

### P2 — Motivation that is felt — `MOT_GOOD` = 227 (11.22%) · `MOT_MSG` = 64 (3.16%) · `CORE_STREAK` = 75 (3.71%)
Motivation layer [U:motivation] 362 (17.89%, high-priority, mean 4.86). The quirky message voice is liked by most (`1339211456`: *"I love the quirky messages when the reminders pop up"*; `1364975878`: *"I loved being called a tiger"*) and disliked by a few (§3.5). Streak aversion is the mechanism reviewers name: `1474061609`: *"I completed a task because I didn't want to break my streak."*

### P3 — Flexible goals and good stats — `CORE_FLEX` = 148 (7.31%) · `STAT_GOOD` = 187 (9.24%)
Tracking-model praise [U:tracking_core] 346 (17.09%, high-priority, mean 4.80). This is the differentiator reviewers name when comparing apps: numeric and negative goals (`1325376912`, `4829199138`), "N times a week" without fixed days (`1333273469`, `1358157955`), percentages by day, week and month (`5501033289`).

### P4 — Reported outcomes — `OUT_LIFE` = 151 (7.46%), high-priority, mean 4.89
Quit smoking (`5836293884`, `9334297173`), sobriety (`1556719109`, `3746772221`, `8850605245`), 26 years of nail-biting (`5361919157`), a year of yoga (`5974956266`), 10K push-ups (`4608093672`), a four-month streak on a years-old habit (`1367237664`).

### P5 — Generous free tier — `PAY_FREE_OK` = 96 (4.74%), very strong, mean 4.86
Free tier discussed [U:free_tier] 148 (7.31%). `1418004252` (au, 5★, Jul 2016): *"some apps make the free version so debilitating you have no choice but to delete or buy). But this app has a great free version"*.

### P6 — Reminders that help — `REM_GOOD` = 97 (4.79%), very strong, mean 4.90
Strong until 2018 and gone after November 2020 (last `6696594352`; E5 0 of 129). The badge countdown is itself a motivator for some (`1435323205`, `2210985599`, `3979432395`).

### P7 — Premium worth it (while it was one-time) — `PAY_WORTH` = 27 (1.33%), meaningful, mean 4.70
22 of the 27 predate June 2018. Post-switch exceptions: `4050502959` (Apr 2019, 12 habits), `5913550190` (May 2020), `12749684903` (Jun 2025: *"Great value to subscribe"*).

### P8 — Community — `COMM_GOOD` = 31 (1.53%), meaningful, mean 4.90
Praised steadily 2015 → 2024 (`2222369246`, `4507852447`, `12098769011`). Its downside is in §3.5.

## 3.7 What is *not* a finding

- **"Too many features."** Only a handful of reviewers want less (`2458999867`, `12577935798`); simplicity praise dominates.
- **Price complaints before June 2018.** Only 2 `PAY_PRICE_HIGH` reviews predate the subscription (`1455910300`, `1470143806`).
- **Security incidents.** No review reports a breach; `PRIV` is about data practices and account pressure.
- **Safety.** No review reports harm. The inverted "not more than" goal (§3.3 N7) matters for quit goals but is reported as a tracking error.
- **Localisation outside Brazil.** 13 non-Brazil `DES_LOC` reviews are spread over 9 storefronts; none clears a country threshold.

---

# PART 4 — WHAT ACTIVE USERS SAY ABOUT THE PRODUCT

## 4.1 The tracking model is the reason people stay

Reviewers describe a specific combination others lacked: numeric and yes/no habits side by side, "not done" distinct from "not logged" (`4182020657`, `4533279715`), weekly goals without fixed days, and back-dating. `2487407176` (us, 5★, Apr 2018, 3 helpful votes): *"I liked that I could have a mix of habits that were weekly and daily. Some that I could only track M-F for work. Some that tracked negative habits like no more than 1 drink on a work night. And counting habits like 8 glasses of water a day."* The gaps they hit sit at the edges of that model: weekly totals (`2212210617`), monthly periods (`10780904796`), custom week starts (`5523298620`), and habits without an end date (`5556749525`).

## 4.2 Reminders: loved early, controllable late, then silent in the data

`REM_GOOD` 97, `REM_CONTROL` 16, `REM_FAIL` 12, `REM_ANNOY` 7. Early iOS builds allowed one reminder per day while Android allowed several (`1719930448`: *"right now all I can set is one reminder per day"*). Later reviewers set several per habit (`5251051111`, `6314169224`). Persistent asks: no reminder for a habit already done (`1411304893`, `1485411857`, `4957470618`), badges that appear without opening the app (`2210985599`, `3394315727`), and push instead of sound-only in silent mode (`10135846337`).

## 4.3 Sync, accounts and the iPad

Reviewers who used more than one device describe the same sequence: pay for sync, open the iPad, find a stretched phone app, and see partial or no data (`1645212180`, `2224962843`, `4174090943`, `4658666246`). `4174090943` (fr, 1★, updated through May 2019): *"Therefore habitbull is a one device app only. And i have paid for pro. Unacceptable."*

## 4.4 Android parity

`USER_ANDROID` = 16 (0.79%, emerging, mean 2.81). The Android version is described as more capable and more reliable: reminders with sound control (`1937297334`), one-tap sync (`1349318323`), fewer crashes (`2224962843`), no flip animations (`7975946217`). `14394403203` (gb, 3★, Aug 2026, Spanish): *"Recientemente cambié mi Android por un iPhone y lamentablemente no funciona bien."* — *EN:* "I recently switched my Android for an iPhone and unfortunately it doesn't work well."

## 4.5 Specialist and sensitive uses

`USE_QUIT` = 46 (2.27%), `USE_MED` = 6 (0.30%), `USE_ADHD` = 3 (0.15%), `USER_COACH` = 7 (0.35%). These users rely on accuracy and privacy most: the "not more than" inversion (§3.3 N7), medication reminders that fail (`2210985599`, `6119183057`), and the launch photo in a NoFap context (`6087663524`). `6961786354` (us, 5★, Feb 2021): *"As someone with executive functioning deficits, I appreciate that this app was designed with care"*.

## 4.6 Small segments recorded for completeness

- **Launch photo** — `DES_SPLASH` 20: 2016–2022; some ask only to turn it off (`1541942639`, `4271501119`).
- **Icon** — `DES_ICON` 6: the switch from the purple icon in 2017 (`1717390815`, `1719063078`) and a clock icon in 2023 (`9898118556`).
- **Registration demanded first** — `5400671798`, `3659840945`.
- **Time zones** — one traveller's completions shift days (`6580945974`).

| `PAY_RESTORE` | negative | 16 | 5.3% | 18 | 88.9% |

## 5.1 Five stars (n = 1,291, 63.78%)

Driven by simplicity (33.2% of 5★ reviews), felt motivation (15.9%), stats (12.9%), comparison with other apps (12.7%), outcomes (10.6%) and flexible goals (9.8%). **18.4% of 5★ reviews carry no product detail** (`META_LOWINFO`). Only 47 of the 1,291 (3.6%) mention paying. Five-star reviews concentrate in E1: 581 of 709 E1 reviews (81.9%) vs 57 of 189 in E4 (30.2%).

## 5.2 Four stars (n = 227, 11.22%)

Four-star reviews are "love it, but". The *but* is dated UI (`DES_UX_NEG` 21, 9.3% of the band), the five-habit cap (`PAY_CAP` 19, 8.4%), new-screen layout (`BUG_COMPAT` 17, 7.5%), crashes (`BUG_CRASH` 16, 7.0%) and platform requests (`PLATFORM_WANT` 11, 4.8%). This is the most actionable band: most items are fixable without changing the core (`3311384193`, `3681275569`, `7212980991`).

## 5.3 Three stars (n = 122, 6.03%)

Crashes dominate (28.7% of the band), then price (`PAY_PRICE_HIGH` 12, 9.8%), cap (9.0%) and layout (9.0%). Three-star reviewers usually still like the app (`3713152341`, `3968794293`, `6696594352`).

## 5.4 Two stars (n = 81, 4.00%)

Crashes (28.4%), paying reviewers (17.3%), churn statements and abandonment (11.1% each). Two-star reviews often mourn a formerly good app: *"Used to be good"* (`5512997233`, `6177722875`).

## 5.5 One star (n = 303, 14.97%)

**Crashes 35.3%, payers 27.1%, support silence 21.8%, account failures 13.2%, sync 11.9%**, churn 10.6%, data loss 9.6%, the pop-up 8.3%, "forced to pay" 5.9%, refund requests 5.6%. One-star reviews are overwhelmingly about **reliability, continuity and money**, not about the tracking model. 23 of them (7.6%) come from long-term users (`USER_LONGTERM`) — a loss of formerly loyal users (`6189130796`, `6948589393`, `9898118556`).

## 5.6 Reviews contradicting their own rating — `META_CONTRA` = 24 (1.19%, meaningful)

19 of the 24 are 5★ reviews describing a failure: crashes (`1857386298`, `3380205850`, `6782765545`), sync (`4999821942`), login (`4606789753`, `4989431292`), the pop-up (`6196323907`, `6215045178`) and a goal-inversion bug (`7768440038`). Three are 1★ reviews containing only praise (`1284895942`, `1401096756`, `1531443175`). One is a 2★ review describing an ADHD "godsend" (`2944590281`), and one is a 3★ "Great!!!" (`1284270584`). Brazil holds 5 of the 24 — Portuguese speakers appear to use stars differently or mis-tap; this cannot be tested. The full list is in Appendix E.

**Implication:** star averages understate the failure rate. In Brazil, 5 of 104 reviews (4.8%) are 5★ complaints.

## 5.7 Theme direction is not fixed by rating

- `PAY_BOUGHT` spans 82×1★ to 47×5★.
- `BUG_COMPAT` has 17 reviews at 4★ — people tolerate a letterboxed app they like.
- `DES_UX_NEG` has 41 of 67 at 4–5★.
- `PAY_CAP` has 44 at 5★ — the limit is often stated neutrally.
- `USER_LONGTERM` splits 23×1★ / 26×5★: the long-term user is both the best advocate and the most betrayed.

# PART 6 — PAID-USER AND UPGRADE ANALYSIS

Scope: all 82 storefronts, 9 Nov 2015 – 6 Aug 2026. **Segment denominator: 165 reviewers who say they paid** (`PAY_BOUGHT`). Segment rates are labelled as such; global counts are shown beside them. **No conversion rate is claimed**: reviews do not reveal how many users paid.

## 6.1 Who the paying reviewers are

**`PAY_BOUGHT` = 165 (8.15%, high-priority, mean 2.56)** · 1/2/3/4/5 = 82/14/11/11/47 · 26 storefronts (us 74, gb 32, br 11, ca 6, in 6, de 5, ru 5).

| Era | Paid reviewers | Share of era | Mean ★ (paid) | 1–2★ | 4–5★ | Data & account among paid | Support silence among paid |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| E1 | 38 | 5.4% | 4.24 | 5 | 31 | 2 | 3 |
| E2 | 37 | 9.2% | 2.78 | 20 | 14 | 18 | 8 |
| E3 | 56 | 9.4% | 1.91 | 42 | 11 | 36 | 20 |
| E4 | 27 | 14.3% | 1.41 | 24 | 1 | 9 | 8 |
| E5 | 7 | 5.4% | 1.86 | 5 | 1 | 4 | 3 |

**Evidence tiers:**
- **Direct purchase statements** (all 165): "I bought premium", "paid for the year", "premium member", "I purchased a lifetime pass".
- **Trial mentions** (`PAY_TRIAL` 8, 0.40%): 5 are 1★, mostly about being charged or cancelling (`3446626954`, `9994781256`). One converted after a two-week trial (`3394315727`).
- **Stated intent, not purchase** (`PAY_INTENT` 19, 0.94%, mean 4.26) — kept out of the paid segment. Several intents were blocked by the product: crashes on purchase (`1923709095`, `2061252128`), hesitation over abandonment (`4916663104`: *"makes me hesitate a little bit before signing up to be billed"*).

## 6.2 What triggers a purchase (in reviewers' own words)

| Trigger | Evidence | IDs |
|---|---|---|
| **Needing more than five habits** | 14 of 165 payers mention wanting more habits (text sub-cut inside `PAY_BOUGHT`), from the first week to 2020 — the leading reason among *satisfied* payers | `1308688562` *"upgraded to premium to be able to have more than five habits"* · `1335999992` · `1339211456` · `1601365268` · `2217520711` · `4050502959` |
| **A low one-time price** | "$4", "worth every penny", "not even very much" | `1313500633` *"I got Premium, was worth $4"* · `1322205409` · `1337793614` · `2078145265` · `2573453386` |
| **Cross-device sync** | 19 of 165 payers tie their purchase to sync (text sub-cut inside `PAY_BOUGHT`) — the most often named reason overall, and mostly in 1–2★ reviews because it failed | `1677887879` *"If you are about to buy it for the sync option. I suggest you keep your money."* · `2105087839` · `4823714493` · `5396158601` |
| **CSV export** | Bought to download data | `2573453386` · `4962257674` |
| **Supporting the developer** | Paying out of goodwill | `1290658855` *"happy to provide my support"* · `1401096756` · `7088475896` *"I moved to paying users right now because I want to appreciate those months it worked great for me"* |
| **A trial that worked** | Converted after the trial | `3394315727` |
| **Accidental purchase** | Via the pop-up or a mistaken upgrade | `6217825775` (risk described) · `11359320730` *"after mistakenly "upgrading""* |

**What payers value once inside** (4–5★ payers, n = 58, segment): `PAY_WORTH` 23, `USER_SWITCH` 18, `DES_CLEAN` 16, `CORE_FLEX` 12, `PAY_CAP` 10, `STAT_GOOD` 10, `OUT_LIFE` 9. Satisfied payers are paying for **capacity on a tool they already trust**, not for new features.

## 6.3 Upgrade barriers

| Barrier | Code | n (global) | Mean ★ | IDs |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|
| Subscription price too high | `PAY_PRICE_HIGH` | 42 | 2.71 | `3713152341` `5394674917` `5544529848` `6857027287` |
| Objection to subscriptions as such | `PAY_SUB_NEG` | 19 | 2.47 | `3092190054` `3114611925` `3544107778` `5598492550` |
| Cap not disclosed / price unclear | `PAY_CONFUSE` | 6 | 2.50 | `2320648884` `3591335378` `6478833374` |
| The purchase itself fails | `PAY_IAP_FAIL` | 14 | 2.57 | `1923709095` `2047546299` `2073281420` |
| Other payers' experiences | (text) | — | — | `6954572953` *"Two online reviews that premium customers had posted convinced me not to."* |
| Signs of abandonment | `DEV_ABANDON` | 42 | 2.07 | `4916663104` `5512997233` |

**Willingness-to-pay statements (text sub-cut inside `PAY_PRICE_HIGH` / `PAY_SUB_NEG`):** reviewers repeatedly name **$5–$10 one-time** as a price they would pay. `3713152341`: *"Would I pay a one-time fee of $5, absolutely, but a yearly fee, nope!"* `5394674917`: *"$5 to $10 as a one-time fee would be reasonable."* `5544529848`: *"I wish it was like $5-10"*. `6436887916` and `6555232508` suggest a $2.99 download price instead of the nag. These are stated preferences, not demand curves.

## 6.4 Post-purchase problems (why payers turn 1★)

Among the 96 payers at 1–2★ (segment): `BUG_CRASH` 39, `SUP_BAD` 38, `SYNC_BUG` 29, `ACCT_BUG` 18, `BUG_DATA` 18, `PAY_REFUND` 17, `PAY_RESTORE` 15, `PAY_REGRET` 11, `USER_CHURN` 10, `DEV_ABANDON` 9, `PAY_REGRESS` 8.

The pattern is a **continuity chain**: a crash or new phone → reinstall → login or reset fails → data and premium gone → email support → no reply → refund request and 1★. `2220539344` (au, 1★, Feb 2018) walks through every step: crash, reinstall, a password that "is wrong", a reset email that never comes, re-registration that "said I don't have the premium function", and then *"I paid 7.99 dollars for this so I want the problem addressed ASAP, otherwise, I have to report problem to Apple App Store and refund."*

**Legacy buyers** — `PAY_REGRESS` = 9 (0.44%, weak, mean 1.33), Dec 2019 → Jan 2025, 7 of 9 at 1★:
- `5303991126` (in, 3★, Dec 2019): *"they offered life time subscription for a certain amount. I bought it. But now they have substantially increased the prices and the people who bought the lifetime pass are no more able to use it the way they had expected."*
- `6217285649` (gb, 1★, Jul 2020): *"I specifically paid for the app because it was a one-off rather than subscription, so I actually feel I should now get my money back"*.
- `12241472265` (es, 1★, Jan 2025): *"Compré hace unos años, y ahora cuando han cambiado a pago mensual, pretenden que pague el pago mensual, olvidándose de que yo ya les había apoyado"* — *EN:* "I bought it years ago, and now that they've switched to monthly payment they expect me to pay monthly, forgetting that I had already supported them."
- Also `5900039933`, `6164110713`, `6300876929`, `6440814309`, `7199060689`, `9315773124`.

## 6.5 Refunds, charges and regret — `PAY_REFUND` = 19 (0.94%, emerging, mean 1.11)

Billing & entitlement failures [U:billing_trust] 71 (3.51%, very strong, mean 1.56). Refund requests run from Jan 2018 (`2105087839`) to Jun 2024 (`11359320730`), and 17 of the 19 come from payers. `PAY_CHARGE` = 7 (0.35%, weak): no way to cancel (`3056682470` *"BEEN TRYING FOR MONTHS TO CANCEL MY SUBSCRIPTION"*, `3628144343`, `3999715752`), charged after a trial (`3446626954`), a trial that can't be cancelled (`9994781256`), and an auto-renewal on an account the user can't log in to (`4606789753`). `PAY_REGRET` = 13 (0.64%, emerging, mean 1.38) — "scam", "fraud", "ripoff" (`4871183371`, `5063663266`, `5872007760`).

## 6.6 Signals that would change a paying user's mind

Reviewers state their own conditions for returning or upgrading their rating:
- **A fix that sticks.** `7097957463` raised their rating when version 1.4.11 worked, and would give 5★ if *"the repetitive splash-ad is removed"*.
- **Sync that works.** *"If it did sub [sync] between devices it would be perfect"* (`7876961629`); `4174090943`.
- **A reply.** `2216664966` *"will update my review based upon their response"*; `4617772381`.
- **Honouring the old purchase.** `5900039933`, `6440814309`.
- **One ask, not every launch.** `7053961857` *"Just ask once and don't make it pop up every time I launch the free app and it gets 5"*.

Six storefronts clear 50 reviews and are analysed individually (§7.3–§7.8): **us 881 · gb 201 · ca 142 · br 104 · de 74 · au 67** — 1,469 reviews (72.58%). The other 76 storefronts hold 555 reviews and are **[limited evidence]**: included in every global figure, with no standalone conclusion drawn. Near-misses: India 41, Russia 36, Netherlands 31. **Per-review weight:** one review moves a de share by 1.35 points and an au share by 1.49 points. Country bands below use the country denominator, and single-digit counts are flagged.

## 7.2 Market groups — defined, not assumed

**High-spend markets.** Membership comes from the `market_tier = "rich"` label in `habit-apps-ranked.json` (the repository's own classification). 27 storefronts in this corpus carry it; together they hold **1,598 reviews (78.95%)**. Because the US dominates, the group is also shown **excluding us (717 reviews)**.

**High-review-volume markets.** No download data is available, so volume is used as a disclosed proxy:
- **(a)** storefronts with ≥50 written reviews — the six above, 1,469 reviews;
- **(b)** by public rating count in the snapshot — us 8,140 · ca 1,373 · gb 1,289 · br 986 · vn 773 · au 725 · in 665 · de 610 · pl 561.

The lists agree on the top six by written volume. Vietnam (773 public ratings, 23 written reviews), India (665, 41) and Poland (561, 16) are high public-volume storefronts with **[limited evidence]** in this corpus.

| Group | Storefronts | Reviews | Mean ★ | Share written Jun 2018+ | Mean ★ Jun 2018+ | `PAY_BOUGHT` | Monetization friction | Subscription backlash | Reliability | Data & account | Neglect | Design praise |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Global | 82 | 2,024 | 4.05 | 45.2% | 3.59 | 8.2% | 8.4% | 4.1% | 21.4% | 7.1% | 7.7% | 28.1% |
| **High-spend (`rich`)** | 27 | 1,598 | 4.07 | 40.3% | 3.52 | 8.4% | 8.9% | 4.4% | 20.2% | 6.9% | 8.1% | 30.2% |
| High-spend excl. us | 26 | 717 | 3.98 | 44.9% | 3.51 | 8.5% | 8.1% | 4.6% | 23.6% | 6.1% | 9.5% | 28.6% |
| **High-review-volume (≥50 written)** | 6 | 1,469 | 4.00 | 41.7% | 3.40 | 9.0% | 9.5% | 4.7% | 21.8% | 7.1% | 7.8% | 30.0% |
| Snapshot `volume` tier | 24 | 375 | 3.97 | 63.7% | 3.75 | 7.2% | 6.1% | 1.9% | 26.4% | 7.7% | 6.4% | 20.3% |
| Not ranked in snapshot | 31 | 51 | 3.98 | 62.7% | 3.88 | 5.9% | 11.8% | 7.8% | 25.5% | 5.9% | 5.9% | 17.6% |
| Sub-50 storefronts combined [limited evidence] | 76 | 555 | 4.16 | 54.6% | 3.97 | 5.9% | 5.8% | 2.3% | 20.5% | 7.0% | 7.6% | 23.1% |

**Reading:**
- The **high-spend group mirrors the global picture** because it is most of the corpus. Excluding the US, reliability failure rises to 23.6% (the UK drives it).
- The snapshot **`volume` tier writes later** (63.7% of its reviews from June 2018 on vs 45.2% globally), crashes more (`BUG_CRASH` 15.5% of the tier) and reports **almost no subscription backlash** (1.9%) or pop-up (`PAY_NAG` 0.5%). That fits price-sensitive users staying inside the free tier — an interpretation, not a measurement. Brazil drives the tier's crash share.
- **Portuguese/Spanish-language storefronts** (177 reviews, [limited evidence] outside Brazil): reliability failure 31.1%, `DES_LOC` 5.1%.
- **German-speaking storefronts** (de, at, ch; 84 reviews): `PAY_NAG` 10.7% — the pop-up landed hardest there relative to size.

- **The UK is the reliability outlier.** `BUG_CRASH` 52 (25.9% of gb, high-priority) and reliability failure 82 (40.8%). Two waves are almost entirely British: **all 12 crash reviews in July 2017** (`1693303166`, `1700324336`, `1700889647`) and **26 of 30 crash reviews in October 2020 – February 2021** (`6583825142`, `6706410264`, `6948589393`). The data cannot show why (device mix, release timing or a regional build).
- **Payers and refunds:** `PAY_BOUGHT` 32 (15.9% of gb), `PAY_REFUND` 9 (4.5%, very strong) — the UK holds 9 of the 19 refund requests.
- **Support:** `SUP_BAD` 24 (11.9%, high-priority).
- **Mean after June 2018: 2.52**, the lowest of any eligible storefront.
- Written-vs-public gap: **1.07★**.

- **November 2018 is Brazil's story.** 23 of Brazil's 34 crash reviews (67.6%) fall in that month (iOS 12, "abre e fecha" — opens and closes). `BUG_CRASH` 34 is 32.7% of br, the highest crash share of any eligible storefront. `3389517105` (1★): *"Sou user premium e ja perdi todos meus dados 2x"* — *EN:* "I'm a premium user and have already lost all my data twice."
- **Localisation is a Brazilian signal:** `DES_LOC` 8 (7.7% of br, high-priority at the country denominator; 8 of the 21 global). Several give 4★ "only because" there is no Portuguese (`1760716102`, `2413516172`, `4422484528`).
- **Low information and contradictions:** `META_LOWINFO` 23 (22.1%) and `META_CONTRA` 5 (4.8%) — five 5★ crash reports (`1857386298`, `3380205850`, `6043777098`).
- 72 of 104 reviews (69.2%) are from June 2018 on, yet `PAY_NAG` is 0 and subscription backlash only 3 (2.9%).
- Written-vs-public gap: **1.18★**, the largest.

- Written-vs-public gap: 0.66★.

## 7.9 Public ratings versus written reviews

| CC | Written reviews | Written mean | Public ratings | Public avg | Written as % of public | Gap |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| us | 881 | 4.14 | 8,140 | 4.67 | 10.8% | 0.53 |
| gb | 201 | 3.37 | 1,289 | 4.44 | 15.6% | **1.07** |
| ca | 142 | 4.32 | 1,373 | 4.62 | 10.3% | 0.30 |
| br | 104 | 3.46 | 986 | 4.64 | 10.5% | **1.18** |
| de | 74 | 3.91 | 610 | 4.57 | 12.1% | 0.66 |
| au | 67 | 4.42 | 725 | 4.56 | 9.2% | 0.14 |
| **Global** | 2,024 | 4.05 | 20,955 | 4.65 | 9.66% | 0.60 |

The gap tracks each storefront's incident exposure: the two storefronts with concentrated crash waves (gb, br) have gaps of more than 1★; the two without (ca, au) stay below 0.35★.

## 7.10 Localisation

`DES_LOC` = 21 (1.04%, meaningful, mean 3.81): br 8, ru 3, jp 2, fr 2, and one each in tw, ch, de, cn, vn, ar. One reviewer says Spanish was available before and then removed (`10228598462`: *"Antes estaba en español y ahora no"*). One Chinese reviewer prefers English as study practice (`3707859371`). Only Brazil clears a country-level signal.

## 7.11 What is not supported by country data

- No standalone conclusion for India (41), Russia (36), the Netherlands (31) or any other sub-50 storefront.
- No claim that UK or Brazilian users are "more critical": their gaps coincide with dated crash waves.
- No spend inference from written-review counts; `rich`/`volume` labels come from the snapshot.
- No East-Asian market analysis: China 24, Korea 11 and Japan 9 reviews, all [limited evidence].

---

# PART 8 — TIME-TREND ANALYSIS

## 8.1 Method

- **Unit:** review `date` (UTC). No version field exists, so release timing is inferred from reviewers' own words and from clustering.
- **Eras** are drawn at dated changes visible in the text, not at calendar years:
  - E1 ends when the July 2017 update crash wave begins.
  - E2 ends at the first subscription review (4 Jun 2018, `2640020936`).
  - E3 ends when the every-launch pop-up appears (5 Jul 2020, `6159632589`).
  - E4 closes a year later, after the 2020–21 crash wave and the pop-up peak.
- **Rates are shares of that era's reviews.** Global counts are in Part 3.
- **Trend labels** (emerging / improving / worsening / persistent) are given only where the era counts support them; E5 (n = 129) is described cautiously.

| Era | Window | Reviews | Mean ★ | 1/2/3/4/5 | 1★ share |
|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|
| E1 | Nov 2015–Jun 2017 · launch, one-time premium (~$4), 5 free habits | 709 | 4.67 | 23/9/17/79/581 | 3.2% |
| E2 | Jul 2017–May 2018 · update crash wave, registration & purchase failures, iPhone X gap | 400 | 3.99 | 63/14/29/51/243 | 15.8% |
| E3 | Jun 2018–Jun 2020 · switch to ~$19.99/yr subscription, sync & support decay | 597 | 3.84 | 108/30/49/75/335 | 18.1% |
| E4 | Jul 2020–Jun 2021 · premium pop-up on every launch, dark-mode & crash regressions | 189 | 2.72 | 79/22/18/13/57 | 41.8% |
| E5 | Jul 2021–Aug 2026 · long tail, nag persists, sporadic bugs | 129 | 3.72 | 30/6/9/9/75 | 23.3% |

| # | Window | What reviewers say | Evidence | Resolution in the data |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I1 | **Jul 2017** | An update makes the app crash on launch (mostly UK); an iPhone 5 build drops support | 47 reviews, mean 3.38, 17×1★; `BUG_CRASH` 12, `BUG_UPDATE` 10, `BUG_COMPAT` 2 · `1673060449` `1683236770` `1700889647` | Fixed by early August (`1707578570`, `1708245143`); August mean 4.70 |
| I2 | **Nov 2017 → Aug 2020** | No iPhone X / notch layout | `BUG_COMPAT` 53 total, first `1923610880`, peak E3 (36) · `3535806272` `4498607051` | Fixed in a December 2020 update (`6718923959`) |
| I3 | **Jan 2018** | Registration spinner, "email invalid", purchase button crashes | 62 reviews, mean 3.50; `ACCT_BUG` 14, `PAY_IAP_FAIL` 10, `BUG_CRASH` 9; 22 of 62 reviews (35.5%) carry `ACCT_BUG` or `PAY_IAP_FAIL` · `2059981427` `2061252128` `2072252724` | `PAY_IAP_FAIL` never recurs after Jan 2018; `ACCT_BUG` continues at a lower level |
| I4 | **Jun 2018** | One-time premium replaced by a ~$19.99/yr subscription; release note says only "Changes" | `PAY_PRICE_RISE` first `2640020936`, `2644893532`; `PAY_PRICE_HIGH` 20 in E3 vs 0 in E2 | Persistent: price complaints continue to 2024 |
| I5 | **Nov 2018** | iOS 12 — app opens and closes (Brazil) | 45 reviews, mean 2.62, 22×1★; `BUG_CRASH` 24, 23 of them in br · `3385427817` `3389236529` `3407975558` | Not stated; Brazil's crash reviews drop to 2 in Dec 2018 |
| I6 | **2019** | Sync dead, support gone, "abandoned" | E3 `SYNC_BUG` 36, `DEV_ABANDON` 29, `SUP_BAD` 36 · `4497695218` `4830530932` `4871183371` | Updates resume in 2020 (`6718923959`), but sync complaints recur to 2022 (`8196540108`) |
| I7 | **Jul 2020** | Full-screen premium pop-up on every launch; X triggers purchase; free users feel locked out; dark mode unreadable; taps mark the wrong day | 68 reviews, **mean 2.09**, 39×1★; `PAY_NAG` 24, `PAY_FORCED` 15, `BUG_UPDATE` 11, `BUG_DARK` 7; 35 of 68 reviews (51.5%) carry `PAY_NAG` or `PAY_FORCED`; 11 reviews on 12 Jul · `6188273678` `6189489425` `6217825775` | `PAY_FORCED` ends Jan 2021; `PAY_NAG` persists (E5 6, last Dec 2024) |
| I8 | **Oct 2020 → Feb 2021** | App crashes on start-up after the 2020 updates; data lost on reinstall; support ticket unanswered | 70 reviews Oct–Feb; `BUG_CRASH` 30, 26 in gb · `6589055391` `6706410264` `6947911333` | Version 1.4.11 fixed it for one reviewer in March 2021 (`7097957463`) |
| I9 | **2026** | Habits can't be deleted or deactivated; login fails after a phone transfer | `13742195758` `13899897825` `14081068315` `14101520315` | Open at corpus end (6 Aug 2026) — 4 reviews, weak |

## 8.5 What the trend data does *not* support

- **No causal claim** that the subscription or the pop-up reduced installs or revenue. Review volume was already falling before June 2018.
- **No 2023–2026 rate comparisons.** 2023–2026 hold 70 reviews in total.
- **No claim that Brazil or the UK are "harsher markets"**: their negative months align with specific incidents.
- **No conclusion about Android** from iOS reviews beyond what reviewers compare (§4.4).

## 8.6 Persistent, improving and disappearing themes

| Theme | Trajectory | Evidence (era shares) | Label |
|---|---|---|---|
| Simplicity praise (`DES_CLEAN`) | Persistent, dips in the incident era | 31.3% → 22.2% → 19.8% → 10.1% → 20.9% | **Persistent** |
| Flexible goals (`CORE_FLEX`) | Persistent | 9.9% → 6.2% → 5.9% → 4.8% → 7.0% | **Persistent** |
| Reminder praise (`REM_GOOD`) | Disappears | 7.6% → 5.5% → 3.2% → 1.1% → 0.0% | **Disappearing** |
| Premium worth it (`PAY_WORTH`) | Disappears after subscription | 2.0% → 2.0% → 0.7% → 0.0% → 0.8% | **Disappearing** |
| Crashes (`BUG_CRASH`) | Worsening to E4, then low | 3.7% → 12.8% → 13.1% → 19.6% → 3.9% | **Improving** in E5 (n = 129; cautious) |
| Support silence (`SUP_BAD`) | Worsening, then persistent | 1.1% → 3.5% → 6.0% → 10.1% → 4.7% | **Persistent** |
| Abandonment (`DEV_ABANDON`) | Recurs whenever updates stall | 0.4% → 0.8% → 4.9% → 1.1% → 3.9% | **Persistent** |
| Sync failure (`SYNC_BUG`) | Peak E3 | 1.3% → 3.0% → 6.0% → 0.5% → 3.1% | **Persistent (recurring)** |
| Subscription price (`PAY_PRICE_HIGH`) | New in E3, peak E4 | 0.3% → 0.0% → 3.4% → 9.5% → 1.6% | **Emerged 2018; improving** |
| Pop-up nag (`PAY_NAG`) | New in E4 | 0 → 0 → 0 → 20.1% → 4.7% | **Emerged 2020; persistent at a lower level** |
| Forced payment (`PAY_FORCED`) | E4 only | 0 → 0 → 0 → 10.6% → 0 | **Resolved** |
| iPhone X layout (`BUG_COMPAT`) | E2–E4 | 0.0% → 2.2% → 6.0% → 4.2% → 0.0% | **Resolved** (Dec 2020) |
| Churn statements (`USER_CHURN`) | Peak E4 | 0.3% → 2.5% → 2.7% → 10.1% → 1.6% | **Worsening to E4** |
| Paid-reviewer share (`PAY_BOUGHT`) | Rises to E4 | 5.4% → 9.2% → 9.4% → 14.3% → 5.4% | Payers write more when things break |

# PART 9 — PRODUCT IMPLICATIONS

Each item names the findings it rests on. **F** = immediate fix · **M** = monetization change to test · **R** = product and retention work · **Q** = research question the corpus cannot answer.

## 9.1 Immediate fixes

| # | Fix | Why (findings) | Success signal to watch |
|---|---|---|---|
| **F1** | **Make sync, restore and login work, and let users recover without support.** Automatic cross-device sync; restore-purchase tied to Apple ID, not an email account; working password reset; data import from CSV. | Data & account union [U:data_account] 143 (7.07%); among payers 69 of 165; `SYNC_BUG` 62; `PAY_RESTORE` 18; `BUG_DATA` 35 at mean 1.31 (§3.3 N2, §6.4) | Fewer `SYNC_BUG`/`ACCT_BUG` reviews; paid-reviewer mean above 3 |
| **F2** | **Remove the every-launch full-screen upsell; make close controls unambiguous so no dismissal can start a purchase.** | `PAY_NAG` 44 (2.17%); `PAY_FORCED` 20 at mean 1.25; July 2020 mean 2.09 (§0.2, §8.4 I7); accidental-purchase reports `6217825775` `11359320730` | `PAY_NAG` returns to 0 |
| **F3** | **Fix tracking correctness:** "not more than" goals saved as "at least"; year-boundary streak resets; edits rewriting history; delete/deactivate habits. | `BUG_STREAK` 11, `CORE_EDIT` 7 (§3.3 N7); quit-goal users `USE_QUIT` 46 | Regression tests on goal direction and week/year boundaries |
| **F4** | **Release safety net:** test every build against current and previous iOS versions and screen sizes; staged rollout; halt on crash spike. | `BUG_CRASH` 197 (9.73%); `BUG_UPDATE` 43; four dated waves (§0.3, §8.4 I1, I5, I8) | No month with >10 crash reviews |
| **F5** | **Answer support and show it:** a working contact route in-app, visible status notes during incidents, meaningful release notes. | `SUP_BAD` 83 (4.10%), mean 1.45; half from payers; "Changes" release note (`2640020936`); broken site (`6247799179`) | `SUP_BAD` share under 1% |
| **F6** | **Registration optional and robust:** accept valid emails, time out gracefully, never block first use. | `ACCT_BUG` 57 (2.82%); Jan 2018 wave (§8.4 I3) | — |
| **F7** | **Finish dark mode and modern layouts; keep them current each iOS cycle.** | `BUG_DARK` 10; `BUG_COMPAT` 53 (§3.3 N5, N9) | — |

## 9.2 Monetization changes to test

| # | Test | Why (findings) | Guardrail |
|---|---|---|---|
| **M1** | **Offer a one-time / lifetime premium beside the subscription, and grant premium to verifiable pre-2018 buyers.** | `PAY_REGRESS` 9 at mean 1.33; `PAY_SUB_NEG` 19; `PAY_PRICE_HIGH` 42; repeated $5–$10 one-time willingness (§6.3, §6.4) | Compare paid-review mean and refund mentions before/after |
| **M2** | **Keep the five-habit free tier; do not shrink it.** Test an upsell shown *at the moment a sixth habit is added*. | `PAY_FREE_OK` 96 (4.74%) praised against 3-habit competitors; more habits is the leading reason among satisfied payers (§0.6, §6.2) | Watch `PAY_CAP` negatives, not just conversions |
| **M3** | **Price the subscription only on features that work** — sync, export, iPad/Watch — and show them working in the paywall. | Premium bundle = capacity + sync + export; sync and export are the broken parts (§2.4) | Sequence after F1 |
| **M4** | **Disclose clearly:** cap, trial length, annual price, how to cancel (inside the app). | `PAY_CONFUSE` 6, `PAY_CHARGE` 7, `PAY_TRIAL` 8 at mean 2.12 (§6.5) | `PAY_CHARGE` reviews to 0 |
| **M5** | **One upsell, not every launch** — frequency-capped and dismissible. | `7053961857` "Just ask once"; E5 still reports the nag (`8292350972`, `9327031706`) | Retention of free users |

## 9.3 Product and retention work

| # | Work | Why (findings) |
|---|---|---|
| **R1** | **Protect and extend the tracking model:** weekly/monthly totals, custom week start, sick/skip days that keep streaks, multiple check-ins per day, optional end dates. | `CORE_FLEX` 148 is the differentiator; gaps `CORE_FREQ` 10, `CORE_SKIP` 5, `CORE_MULTI` 5, `CORE_ENDDATE` 2 (§3.6, §4.1) |
| **R2** | **Reach the rest of the Apple ecosystem:** widget with one-tap check-off, Apple Watch app, real iPad layout, then web. | Device reach union [U:device_reach] 113 (5.58%); churn statements tied to it (`2013414846`, `3257491999`) |
| **R3** | **Close Android–iOS parity gaps** in reminders and sync. | `USER_ANDROID` 16 (§4.4) |
| **R4** | **Smarter reminders:** only for undone habits, several per habit, push in silent mode, badge without opening. | `REM_CONTROL` 16, `REM_ANNOY` 7, `REM_FAIL` 12; reminder praise disappeared (§4.2, §8.6) |
| **R5** | **Retire brand irritants:** make the launch photo optional or remove it; offer a neutral message tone. | Brand irritants [U:brand_irritants] 30 (1.48%) (§0.8) |
| **R6** | **Localise Portuguese (pt-BR) first**, then Russian. | `DES_LOC` 21; br 8 (7.7% of br) (§7.6, §7.10) |
| **R7** | **Forum hygiene:** topic filters and blocking of shaming replies. | `COMM_NEG` 8 vs `COMM_GOOD` 31 (§3.5) |
| **R8** | **Throttle the rating prompt.** | `META_PROMPT` 9 (§1.7) — weak signal, included on review-integrity grounds |

## 9.4 Research questions this corpus cannot answer

| # | Question | Why it matters |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | How many one-time buyers were affected by the 2018 switch, and how many still use the app? | Sizes the cost of M1 |
| Q2 | Did the July 2020 pop-up raise paid conversions enough to offset churn and rating loss? | Reviews show only the cost side |
| Q3 | Is the sync backend healthy today? Reviewers claimed "servers are dead" in 2019 | F1 scope |
| Q4 | Are the UK (2017, 2020) and Brazil (2018) crash waves device-, OS- or build-specific? | F4 test matrix |
| Q5 | How much of the short 5★ volume comes from the in-app prompt? | Interpreting the public 4.65 |

## 9.5 What to do first

1. **F1 + F6** — continuity for paying users. It is the largest paid-segment failure and a prerequisite for M3.
2. **F2 + M5** — remove the every-launch upsell. It is the cheapest change with the clearest rating effect in the data.
3. **F4 + F5** — a release process and a support channel, so the next wave is caught and answered.
4. **M1** — a lifetime option and honouring legacy buyers.
5. **F3** — correctness of goals, which quit-goal users depend on.
6. **R1, R2** — extend the model users already praise, onto the devices they already own.

---

# PART 9.1 — EVIDENCE APPENDIX

## A. Counting rules

1. **Denominator:** all global percentages divide by 2,024; country rates by that storefront's count; era and year rates by that period's count; payer rates by 165.
2. **Non-exclusive themes:** a review carries every theme it supports. Family and composite unions count each review once.
3. **Bands** follow the analysis spec: < 0.1% ignore · 0.1–0.5% weak · 0.5–1% emerging · 1–3% meaningful · 3–5% very strong · > 5% high-priority.
4. **Mean ★** is the arithmetic mean of `rating` across the reviews in a theme.
5. **Lift** = (segment rate) ÷ (global rate).
6. **Text sub-cuts** are counted only inside a hand-coded theme and are always labelled as such.
7. **No review was excluded, down-weighted or de-duplicated.**
8. **Composite unions** are written as `[U:name]` followed by their count; their codes are listed in §3.2.

## D. Judgement calls and known error risk

1. **`PAY_BOUGHT`** includes one-time buyers, subscribers, lifetime-pass holders and trial converts who say they paid. It excludes stated intent (`PAY_INTENT`) and hypotheticals ("even if you upgrade"). Brazil's "Don't buy it" with "for the price" (`1468601578`) is coded as a purchase; it is the most uncertain inclusion.
2. **`MOT_GOOD` vs `OUT_LIFE`:** "keeps me on track / accountable" is `MOT_GOOD`; a concrete result (quit, streak length, habit formed, "changed my life") is `OUT_LIFE`. Short enthusiastic claims ("Life changing") were given `OUT_LIFE`. This is the boundary most likely to move counts (±15%).
3. **`DES_CLEAN` vs `META_LOWINFO`:** a named property ("easy", "simple", "user friendly") gets `DES_CLEAN`; "great app" gets `META_LOWINFO`.
4. **`BUG_CRASH` vs `BUG_OTHER`:** crash, closes, won't open, shuts down → `BUG_CRASH`; "doesn't work" or "broken" without crash wording → `BUG_OTHER`.
5. **`PAY_NAG` vs `PAY_FORCED`:** `PAY_FORCED` only when the reviewer says they could not use the app or reach their data without paying. Some of these may be the same pop-up described more strongly; both codes are kept where both are described.
6. **`PAY_REGRESS` vs `PAY_RESTORE`:** `PAY_REGRESS` when an earlier one-time/lifetime purchase is not honoured under the new model; `PAY_RESTORE` when a purchase is simply not recognised after reinstall or a new phone.
7. **`BUG_COMPAT`** covers both the iPhone X+ layout and old devices dropped by an update.
8. **`USER_SWITCH`** is applied to any comparison with other habit apps ("best I've tried"), not only to reviewers who moved from a named app.
9. **Contradictions** are coded only where the text and the rating clearly conflict. A 4★ review titled "Awesome!" that reports constant crashes (`1424742582`) was *not* coded.
10. **Storefront and language:** reviews are assigned to the storefront they were posted in, whatever their language.

**Known error risk overall:** the headline clusters were re-read in full after coding — the subscription switch (§0.2), the July 2020 pop-up (§8.4 I7), the four crash waves (§0.3), payers' continuity failures (§6.4) and legacy buyers (§6.4) — and reviewers describe them in plain words. The risk concentrates in the soft-positive boundaries above and in the request codes. **No recommendation in Part 9 rests on a single theme smaller than 5 reviews**, except R8 (review integrity) and F3 (tracking correctness), which say so.
